# STATUS.md

The single source of truth for execution progress against IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md. The agent updates rows. Only a human sets `done`.

Statuses: `todo`, `in_progress`, `in_review`, `blocked`, `done`, `skipped`. Skipping requires a note and human sign-off. The rules are in EXECUTION-GUIDE.md §5.

**105 tasks.**

Three real bugs found by running it, all invisible to helm lint: a pre-install hook cannot reference the chart's ServiceAccount or its Secrets, because Helm creates every pre-install hook before any normal resource, so the first install failed twice; and the generated encryption key was 32 characters rather than 32 bytes, which the key ring refused at boot exactly as designed. The Phase 1 exit checklist is recorded in docs/design/p1-exit-checklist.md with evidence per line and four caveats written down rather than ticked: the action registry is real but holds two actions, authorisation is a seam with no bindings until P2-T01, mail has never reached a real mailbox, and instance settings writes are a stated exception to the Operation pipeline. Human confirmed four decisions before code: CI installs the chart into a real kind cluster and registers a user, because a chart can lint perfectly and fail on install and this repository has been bitten four times by gates that pass while checking nothing; the chart deploys no database and requires an external one, matching PLAN §121 and avoiding a third-party subchart whose backups nobody owns; images publish to ghcr.io on a version tag and are signed with cosign keyless, so there are no signing keys to store or rotate; and the chart generates its secrets on first install, reads them back on upgrade so a helm upgrade never rotates a key underneath stored data, and accepts an existingSecret for operators with their own secret manager |

### Phase 1 hardening (between P1-T10 and P2-T01)

Not a numbered task. A review of the whole of Phase 1 after P1-T10 merged, on
branch `chore/p1-hardening`, fixing what would be more expensive to fix once
Phase 2 built on it. Six fixes, each with a test that fails without it.

| # | What was wrong | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The Operation pipeline applies `app.workspace_id` and `app.user_id` together, and Postgres combines permissive policies with OR, so every read inside every operation also returned the acting user's rows from every other workspace. A test asserted that union and called it correct | Migration 0008 scopes the two cross-workspace policies to transactions that name no workspace, and aligns their soft-delete predicates. The switcher and the provisioning membership check both use `withUser`, which sets no workspace, so both are unchanged |
| 2 | `./openokr rotate-key` had never worked. It ran a file the image did not contain, and `NODE_PATH` was exported by the entrypoint alone so nothing reached it through `docker compose exec` | The migrator layer now carries the core and config sources with the workspace links rebuilt, plus the two packages Next compiles away rather than tracing out. The smoke test rotates the key and then proves the instance still reads its own data. Image 205MB to 235MB |
| 3 | `OPENOKR_DOMAIN=... ./openokr up` wrote `BETTER_AUTH_URL=http://localhost` and kept it forever, because the helper read `OPENOKR_URL`, which nothing sets, and secrets are generated once. Passkeys bind to that origin | The origin follows `OPENOKR_DOMAIN`, and is reconciled on every `up` rather than only on the run that generated the secrets. It says when passkeys will need re-enrolment |
| 4 | The outbox rule did not cover `apps/`, and a direct mail send was already sitting there. One mention of `runOperation` exempted an entire file. Raw SQL writes and side effects reached through a call expression were both invisible | The rule covers `apps/`, exempts only writes lexically inside a pipeline call, and sees `tx.execute(sql\`update ...\`)` and `mailerFrom(s).send(...)`. It immediately found two real writes in provisioning, now marked with the operation they run inside |
| 5 | The migration linter accepted a policy on `invitations` as a policy on `invitation`, read `-- openokr:hard-delete is deliberately absent` as the marker being present, allowed `using (true)` on a workspace table, and skipped a directory it could not find | All four closed. Directories are now counted individually, so a renamed migrations directory fails instead of passing on the fixtures alone |
| 6 | SMTP error redaction covered the password and `base64(password)`, but AUTH PLAIN sends `base64(NUL user NUL pass)`. Whether the password survived depended on byte alignment, so it leaked for roughly two usernames in three | Every encoding is redacted, and any base64 left standing is decoded and checked. Proved against a server that quotes the rejected AUTH line back |

Also: CodeQL scoped away from the reference HTML that accounted for all forty
open alerts and none of the product code; `engines` pinned to `>=22 <23`
because every entry point runs `--experimental-strip-types`; six commands that
CLAUDE.md documented but that do not exist moved into a table naming the task
that delivers each; the release workflow gained the sign-off, chart and compose
gates it was missing and lost an `id-token` permission a verifier should not
hold; the compose smoke test stopped pinning the migration count; and the four
placeholder package tests now say they are wiring checks rather than coverage.

Five more findings are recorded on the Phase 2 rows they belong to rather than
fixed here: realtime reconnection and the outbox dead-letter path (P2-T06),
cache eviction (P2-T09), the port lifecycle contract (closed early, see the
cross-phase gap review below, before P2-T13 needed it), the job queue
restart (P2-T17), and local-disk content types (P2-T05).

### Cross-phase gap review (between P2-T12 and P2-T13, 2026-08-10)

Not a numbered task. The human asked for an audit of gaps between Phase 1 and
Phase 2, then asked for all of them to be fixed directly rather than left for
whichever later task would otherwise have carried them. On branch
`chore/close-phase1-gaps`. Four fixes.

| # | What was wrong | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The port lifecycle contract had no agreement: `JobQueue` and `Realtime` declared `stop()`, the other six ports declared nothing, `SmtpMailer.close()` existed but was not on the `Mailer` port, and `Adapters.close()` only ever called two of eight drivers. Flagged since P1-hardening and carried on the P2-T13 row as "settle the contract before a ninth driver with long-lived HTTP clients joins" — which is exactly what P2-T13's AI provider drivers are | Every port (`Cache`, `Mailer`, `Search`, `Channel`, `FileStorage`, `AIProvider`, plus the two that already had it) now declares `stop(): Promise<void>`. Six drivers that own nothing to release no-op it; `SmtpMailer.stop()` wraps its existing `close()`. `Adapters.close()` calls all eight. A driver added later — the first AI provider driver among them — is shut down by construction rather than by remembering to wire it in |
| 2 | Sign-in lockout has been enforced since P1-T05 without an audit entry, first because the audit spine did not exist (closed at P1-T07), then because `audit_events.workspace_id` is `not null` and a failed sign-in has no workspace to attach to — a fact about an email address and a caller address, resolved before any membership is known, that can implicate zero, one or several workspaces. Carried through P1-T07's row, then P2-T09's, each time deferred to "the P8-T03 open question" | A second, parallel chain: `instance_audit_events` (migration 0014), hash-chained the same way `audit_events` is but with no `workspace_id` and one sequence for the whole instance, read and written through the same `app.instance_admin` transaction-local flag `system_settings` already uses. `packages/core/src/audit/instance-chain.ts` is its hashing and append logic, parallel to `audit/chain.ts` rather than sharing it, because an instance row has no actor, target or workspace for that type to require. Better Auth's own rate limiter rejects a request from inside its router, before any hook, `onAPIError` or `onResponse` callback ever runs, so there is no extension point inside it that ever sees the 429 it produces (confirmed by tracing `better-call`'s router, not assumed); `apps/web/app/api/auth/[...all]/route.ts` wraps the handler instead and writes the entry when the response is a 429. Deliberately not recorded: the attempted email address, on §8.2's own "minimal personal data in logs" line — the path and the caller's address already show a lockout happened, where, and from where |
| 3 | TECHNICAL-PLAN §1's package dependency table never listed `config` for any package, but `packages/core`, `packages/db` and `apps/web` all already reach it (mostly from command-line entry points: `migrate.ts`, `data-change.ts`, `verify-audit.ts`, `rotate-keys.ts`, plus `apps/web`'s own boot check). First noticed at P1-T02 ("confirm the TECHNICAL-PLAN §1 table allows it") and never confirmed in the four tasks since | Added a note under the table: `config` sits underneath it rather than inside it, available to every package the way `zod` is, because it is the shared environment and lint schema rather than a domain dependency. No code changed; the table was stale, not the imports |
| 4 | Two stale task citations, found while fixing the above and while working on P2-T12: IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md's P2-T16 cited `AI-NATIVE-PLAN.md §1.7`, which does not exist (§1 has no subsections); CLAUDE.md's "commands that do not exist yet" table attributed `pnpm gen:contract` to P2-T09, which is unrelated (security baseline), when `packages/core`'s own registry names P5-T07 onward | Corrected both directly, with the human's explicit go-ahead. §1.7 is now §4 ("Budgets and limits"); `gen:contract` now cites P5-T07 (REST/OpenAPI/CLI, where the drift check it depends on is built), noting P5-T06 and P5-T09 as the chat-command and MCP-tool-catalogue projections of the same registry. Committed separately as `chore/fix-ai-native-plan-citations`, ahead of this row |

Two items from the audit were surfaced but deliberately not treated as bugs to
fix: P1-T08 and P1-T10 are still `in_review`, not `done` — CLAUDE.md reserves
setting a task `done` for a human, so this is not the agent's row to change,
only to flag. And mail has never been tested against a real SMTP server
(carried since the P1-T09 exit checklist) — verifying that needs either a
live external account or a local test SMTP catcher, neither of which this
sandbox can add or prove without a human decision on the dependency.

**Verified** under Node 22: typecheck (`packages/adapters`, `packages/db`,
`packages/core`, `apps/web`), Biome, the boundary gate, the migration linter
and the soft-delete lint all pass. `packages/config`'s own suite and, for the
first time, `apps/web`'s own Vitest suite (it needs no database, unlike
`packages/core`/`packages/db`) both ran and passed here, 16 tests including
four new ones proving the lockout-audit wrapper: a 429 gets exactly one audit
call with the right path and address, an ordinary response gets none, a
failing audit write still returns the 429 rather than a 500, and the address
falls back from `x-forwarded-for` to `x-real-ip` to a placeholder. **Not
verified:** `packages/core/test/instance-audit.test.ts` and the `stop()`
additions to `packages/adapters/test/{drivers,smtp}.test.ts` — no
Docker/Postgres in this sandbox for those two packages, same gap as every
prior Phase 2 row.

### Cross-phase gap review 2: the first real database run (2026-08-10)

Not a numbered task. This machine has a real native PostgreSQL 18.4 install
(the user's own, not Docker, which is not installed here), so for the first
time in this project's history `packages/core` and `packages/db`'s own test
suites actually ran end to end rather than typechecking alone. Result:
`packages/core` 370/370 passing, `packages/db` 97/101 (the remaining 4 need a
real PgBouncer at the pooling-spike test's own fixed port, absent on this
machine, an environment gap rather than a bug — see its own row). Every prior
Phase 1 and Phase 2 row's "not verified: the Vitest suite" note is closed by
this pass, and it surfaced real defects no static check could have caught.

| # | What was wrong | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | An ordinary invited member held no access binding on the workspace's own context at all. `insertWorkspaceAndMember` (`packages/core/src/workspaces/provisioning.ts`) bound only the founding member's own `member` group at `full`; `workspace_standard`'s group was created but never bound to anything. Every non-founding member resolved to access level 0 on the workspace context — unable to read the overview, unable to update their own profile, unable to do anything a workspace-scoped action gates on — and this went undetected because every existing test either used the founding member or explicitly granted `full` first | `workspace_standard` now gets a real binding at `edit` on the workspace's own context, at provisioning time, for every new workspace. A data-change script (`0002_backfill_workspace_standard_binding.ts`) grants it to every workspace provisioned before this fix. `pnpm db:change` picks up both scripts now |
| 2 | Seven write actions declared `access: ACCESS_LEVELS.view`, one full step below the registry's own invariant ("a write action that only asked for view would be a silent escalation," `test/registry.test.ts`) — never caught because no test ever exercised an ordinary, non-`full` member against any of them. `people.updateOwnProfile`, `blobs.prepareUpload`, `blobs.claimUpload`, `notifications.markRead`, `notifications.snooze`, `notifications.updateSettings`, `subscriptions.toggle` | All seven bumped to `edit`, the level `workspace_standard` now actually grants, so an ordinary member's self-service writes work for the first reason to hold rather than the coincidence of getting through at level 0 (which none of them, in fact, ever did) |
| 3 | `invitations.acceptLink`/`joinByTrustedDomain` are `bootstrap: true` operations, which `runOperation` treats as `full` unconditionally since there is no member yet to hold a real level — so their own declared `access: view` was always cosmetic, but still tripped the registry's blanket invariant | Declared `edit` instead, for the invariant's own sake; behaviour is unchanged, since `bootstrap` already overrides whatever is declared |
| 4 | A genuinely open design question, flagged by name in this file's P2-T02 row and recorded as *current, untested-until-now behaviour rather than a confirmed decision*: does a guest (or an agent, or a placeholder) inherit the same blanket `workspace_standard`/`space_standard` tier a human member does? Fixing #1 forced an answer, since a blanket binding now genuinely exists to inherit. **Put to the human directly, who confirmed:** no. `resolveMemberAccessLevel` and `accessScopeFilter` (`packages/core/src/access/reads.ts`) now reach the two blanket tiers only for `kind = 'human'`; a personal (`member`-kind) binding still reaches anyone, kind notwithstanding. Matches AI-NATIVE-PLAN §1.3's "no service account with ambient authority" for an agent, and ordinary "guest" semantics — a tighter answer than the one this codebase had been running with, unverified, since P2-T01 |
| 5 | This machine's native Postgres cluster initialised with `server_encoding = WIN1252`, not UTF8 — found when a CJK-content provisioning test failed to insert a real character outside WIN1252's repertoire. Latin-1 content (English, Bahasa Indonesia) is unaffected; anything else is not, in the real running app too, not only in tests | The user's own `openokr` database was dropped and recreated with explicit `ENCODING 'UTF8'` (data loss accepted; it held only this session's own dev/test account) and migrated again. Separately, `packages/test-support/src/db-harness.ts`'s own test-template `CREATE DATABASE` now requests `ENCODING 'UTF8' LC_COLLATE 'C' LC_CTYPE 'C' TEMPLATE template0` explicitly rather than inheriting whatever `template1` happened to be, so the test database stops depending on the host cluster's own default at all |
| 6 | `runDataChanges` (`packages/db/src/data-change.ts`) returned the *lifetime* cumulative `rowsChanged`/`batches` for a script in its outcome, not what that specific call actually did — invisible until a real resume-after-crash test proved a call that changed 2 rows reported 4, because the ledger's prior total from an earlier, interrupted run was folded in | Split into a lifetime total (still written to the `_data_changes` ledger, for anyone querying it directly) and a per-call session total, now what `runDataChanges`'s return value and `pnpm db:change`'s own printed line report — what an operator re-running a script across several sessions actually wants to know |
| 7 | Two real, if minor, product-code bugs in the data-change scripts themselves, both `max(uuid_column)` — Postgres's `max()` aggregate has no `uuid` overload, so both scripts' own cursor-advancement query would fail outright the first time either ever ran against a real database: the shipped `0001_backfill_member_timezone.ts`, and this review's own new `0002_backfill_workspace_standard_binding.ts` (item 1) written before this was caught | Both rewritten as `order by <col> desc limit 1` against the already-ordered batch CTE, the same value without the unsupported aggregate |
| 8 | A cluster of test-fixture defects across five files, none in product code, all invisible without a real database: literal non-UUID strings (`"matrix-2"`, `"doc-3"`, `"secret-doc"`, …) passed as `uuid`-typed columns (`access_contexts.resource_id`, `activities.subject_id`) in `access-reads.test.ts`, `notifications.test.ts` and `activities.test.ts`; a synthetic `"test-aggregate"` subject type fed to `getAccessScoped`, whose `SUBJECT_RESOLVERS` map is deliberately exhaustive and fail-closed, silently resolving every recipient to "no access" through `resolveRecipients`'s own `.catch()`; `instance-audit.test.ts` calling `DELETE` on the append-only `instance_audit_events` table where the migration's own comment says `TRUNCATE` is the one deliberately open path; `invitations.test.ts` inserting two `users` rows with the same email to simulate "the same address, a different account," which `users.email`'s own unique constraint has never allowed; and `operation-pipeline.test.ts`'s own hand-rolled `workspace.renamed` activity payload missing the `from` field the catalogue schema requires | Fixed each in place: real generated UUIDs (`randomUUID()`) wherever a resourceId/subjectId needs one, kept correlated across the calls that must agree on it; `"blob"` substituted for `"test-aggregate"` wherever `getAccessScoped`/`resolveRecipients` actually resolves by type (not wherever a stored `contextId` is used directly, which needs no registered type and must avoid `"blob"` specifically, since `queryFeed`'s own liveness filter checks a `blob` subject against the real table); `TRUNCATE` for cleanup, kept as `DELETE` for the one test asserting delete itself is refused; a second, distinct email for the invitations test; `from` added to the pipeline test's payload |
| 9 | `access-model.test.ts`'s own provisioning-shape assertions (exactly one binding; workspace_standard bound a second time inside a later test) were written before item 1 existed and never re-checked against it | Updated to expect the founding member's `full` binding and workspace_standard's new `edit` binding as the two rows they now are, and removed the later test's now-redundant, now-colliding re-bind of workspace_standard on the same context |

**Verified** under Node 22, against the user's own real native Postgres for
`packages/core` and `packages/db` specifically (a first for both): `pnpm
typecheck`, Biome on every touched file, the boundary gate (232 files), the

a real PgBouncer this machine does not have running, `ECONNREFUSED` to its
fixed port, not a code defect). **Not verified:** `packages/adapters`,

re-exercised this pass; only the two packages whose own Vitest projects this
review actually ran are covered by the "first real run" claim above.

| P2-T01 | Access model: contexts, bindings, groups | done | task/p2-t01-access-model | 2026-08-09 | Closes both P1-T06 and P1-T07 follow-ups: workspace provisioning now gives the workspace its own access context and `workspace_standard` group, and gives the first member a `member` group with a `full` binding on that context, atomically in the same transaction. Four tables (`access_contexts`, `access_groups`, `access_group_memberships`, `access_bindings`), migration 0009, all with the tenant floor and RLS. `access_group_memberships` is schema-only for now: nothing writes to it until `space_standard` groups exist at P3-T01, because workspace-wide membership is read off `workspace_members` directly rather than enumerated. `derivePrivacy` (packages/core/src/access/privacy.ts) is pure and untested against a stored value by design, matching "never a stored boolean". `can()` and the access-aware getter that actually read these tables are P2-T02; every active member still resolves to `full` until then. Reusable `ensureContext`/`ensureWorkspaceStandardGroup`/`ensureMemberGroup`/`bindGroup` helpers in packages/core/src/access/contexts.ts are for later Operations to call when they create their own aggregate; provisioning is the only caller today. Verified: typecheck, Biome lint, the migration linter (10 files), the boundary gate (130 files) and the soft-delete lint (6 tables, 86 files) all pass under Node 22, run directly since this sandbox's default Node is 20 and pnpm's own corepack shim would not run at all. Not verified: the actual test suite. This sandbox has no Docker and no reachable Postgres, and every package's Vitest project opens a real database in global setup, so packages/core/test/access-model.test.ts and the existing provisioning suite could not be executed here; `derivePrivacy`'s cases were checked by hand outside Vitest instead. A human needs to run `pnpm db:up && pnpm test` before merging. **2026-08-10, once that test suite finally ran for real:** `workspace_standard`'s own binding was missing entirely — see "Cross-phase gap review 2" below, item 1, for the fix and its backfill |
| P2-T02 | can() + access-aware reads | done | task/p2-t02-can-access-reads | 2026-08-09 | Closes both inherited follow-ups. `resolveActor` in operation.ts now resolves the member's real level on the workspace's own context instead of hardcoding `full`; `ResolvedActor.level` is a plain number rather than `AccessLevel` because zero is a real answer. `workspace.overview` now calls `getAccessScoped` to authorise, with one remaining raw read of its own display fields marked `openokr:allow-raw-read`. Built: `resolveMemberAccessLevel` (member's own group, workspace_standard, and any space_standard group they hold a live `access_group_memberships` row in — maximum wins), `resolveAnonymousAccessLevel` (the anonymous group alone, kept separate rather than making `can()`'s memberId optional), `can()`, `getAccessScoped` (not-found on missing resource, insufficient level or suspension alike), `accessScopeFilter` (a composable EXISTS clause for listing), and the fail-closed `resolveSubjectContext` with one entry so far (`workspace`). Extracted `OperationError` into its own module (`operations/errors.ts`) to break a circular import between operation.ts and the new access/reads.ts. Extended `packages/config/src/boundaries.ts` with a fourth rule, `protected-read-outside-getter`, covering `workspaces` today; found and fixed a real bug in the same change while wiring it up: `statementStartLine`, reused from the mutation rule, stops at the first `{`/`}` walking backward, which is the closing brace of a preceding multi-line `.select({...})` argument rather than the statement's own start, so the escape marker has to sit directly above `.from(...)` for this rule rather than above the statement. A regression test locks in the correct placement. Added a test fixture table, `access_probes` (mirroring `tenant_probes` from the P1-T03 spike), to exercise `accessScopeFilter` against several rows at once; never shipped. **Open question for a human:** the guest kind inherits the `workspace_standard` tier exactly like a human member today, because nothing in TECHNICAL-PLAN §4.1 or P2-T01 excludes it. Is that correct, or should a guest only ever reach what a personal binding names? Recorded and tested as current behaviour in packages/core/test/access-reads.test.ts, not silently assumed. **Not verified:** the Vitest suites. See "Cross-phase gap review 2" below, item 4 |
| P2-T03 | People: profiles, manager chain, lifecycle | done | task/p2-t03-people-lifecycle | 2026-08-09 | No new migration: `workspace_members` already carried every field this task needed since P1-T06. Built `packages/core/src/people/manager-chain.ts` (cycle detection, possible-managers, the org-chart tree, all depth-capped rather than trusting the invariant unconditionally) and `people/lifecycle.ts` (the last-owner check, `stripBindings` for guest conversion), registered as actions in `actions/people.ts`: `updateOwnProfile`, `updateMember`, `suspend`, `restore`, `convertToGuest`, `erase`, `directory`, `orgChart`, `possibleManagers`. Suspension needed no binding changes at all: `resolveMemberAccessLevel` and `resolveActor` already exclude a non-active member (P2-T02), so suspend and restore are exactly the status flip and nothing else. **Two readings recorded for a human to confirm, neither assumed silently:** (1) nothing in TECHNICAL-PLAN §4.1 names an owner role, so "the last owner" is read as *the last active member holding `full` on the workspace's own context*, checked by walking every other active member's resolved level rather than counting bindings, since a member can reach `full` through more than one tier. (2) The self-versus-others split for profile fields is this task's own line: self edits timezone, avatar and bio (personal); an admin with `full` edits name, title and manager (org-structural). Erasure sets the row to a placeholder name with `bio`/`title`/`avatar`/`timezone`/`userId` cleared and `status` forced to `suspended`, keeping the row itself as the stable identity later authorship still points at, and returns the prior profile as the operation's own result rather than writing it anywhere. **Deliberately not built:** the S-33 screen itself (directory UI, profile page, org chart rendering). IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN lists Depends on: P2-T02 only, but there is no app shell or design system to build a real page into yet (P2-T10); building one now would be throwaway, the way the workspace switcher's UI was deferred from P1-T06 to P2-T10 for the same reason. The three read actions (`directory`, `orgChart`, `possibleManagers`) exist and are tested; only their rendering is deferred, and this row is that follow-up. Bio remains unvalidated JSON: the shared rich-text validator TECHNICAL-PLAN §4 requires is P2-T11, not yet built, so `bio` is accepted loosely rather than ahead of that module. **Verified** under Node 22 (see P2-T01/P2-T02 rows for why): typecheck, Biome, the boundary gate, the migration linter and the soft-delete lint all pass. **Not verified:** packages/core/test/people.test.ts, same Docker/Postgres gap as every other Phase 2 row so far. Needs `pnpm db:up && pnpm test` before merging |
| P2-T04 | Invitations | done | task/p2-t04-invitations | 2026-08-10 | Closes the P1-hardening follow-up: `invitations.createPersonalLink` is the first caller to actually go through the outbox rather than a direct send, topic `invitation.email`. Migration 0010 adds `invite_links` (mode `workspace`/`personal`, SHA-256 token hash, `email` set only for `personal`, `allowed_domains`, `use_count`/`max_uses`, `expires_at`, `revoked_at`, `member_id` filled in at acceptance). All three joining paths (`invitations.acceptLink` for both link modes, `invitations.joinByTrustedDomain` against `workspaces.settings.trustedEmailDomains`, already resolvable since P1-T06) funnel through one `provisionMemberForInvite` in `packages/core/src/invitations/provisioning.ts`, idempotent on an existing live membership. Both acceptance actions are `bootstrap` operations — the accepting person has no member row yet — which widened the doc comment on `OperationSpec.bootstrap` in operation.ts from "creates the workspace" to "creates the workspace, or creates this member's membership in it", since it now has two callers instead of one. **Two things recorded for a human, neither decided silently:** (1) no invite carries its own access level (TECHNICAL-PLAN's `invite_links` row has no such column), so every joining path grants the same default (`edit`) `defineWriteAction` itself defaults to; a per-invite level is a natural follow-up once someone needs to invite at `view` or `comment` instead. (2) The raw invite token has to travel through the outbox payload for the email to be sendable at all, since the column stores only its hash and the raw value exists nowhere else after this transaction. Outbox rows are not deleted after delivery today, so the raw token stays readable in the table past the point the invite is used or expires — worth a look at P7-T03, not fixed here since it would mean inventing outbox retention this task wasn't asked to build. Also fixed on the way: the migration's first draft indexed `token_hash` globally, on the reasoning that a token might be looked up before its workspace is known; it doesn't, an invite URL names its workspace (by slug) alongside the token, so acceptance runs with the tenant floor already applied like any other request, and the index is workspace-scoped like every other one in this schema. **Verified** under Node 22: typecheck, Biome, the boundary gate, the migration linter and the soft-delete lint all pass. **Not verified:** packages/core/test/invitations.test.ts — no Docker/Postgres in this sandbox, same gap as every prior Phase 2 row. Needs `pnpm db:up && pnpm test` before merging |
| P2-T05 | Files and blobs | done | task/p2-t05-files-blobs | 2026-08-10 | Closes the P1-hardening follow-up: removed `LocalDiskStorage`'s process-local `#contentTypes` map outright, since nothing ever read it. Content type is now sourced durably from the `blobs` table (migration 0011), which is what should have been holding it all along. Migration adds `status` value `pending` beyond the three TECHNICAL-PLAN names (`ok`/`scanning`/`quarantined`): the gap between prepare and claim, and what the (unwired) orphan cleanup query targets — flagged as an extension of the terse spec, not a silent change. New workspace setting `storageQuotaBytes` (§4.14), default 5 GiB; TECHNICAL-PLAN names no figure. Each blob is its own protected aggregate (`resourceType: "blob"`), with the uploader given `full` through their own member group at prepare time, and `blobs.getForDownload` resolves it through `getAccessScoped` rather than trusting `author_member_id`. "Exactly one warning" is read as one per crossing of ninety percent, not one per upload while already over it: `checkQuota` compares the total before and after this specific upload. **Deliberately not built, and flagged for a human rather than silently skipped:** image re-encoding, a thumbnail worker and a virus-scan hook all need a new runtime dependency (an image library at minimum) that CLAUDE.md requires asking about first. `claimBlob` accepts an optional `requiresScan` flag that moves a claim to `scanning` instead of `ok`, so the state machine will not need to change shape once a scanner exists — scaffolding, not a built capability. The orphan cleanup job itself is the same story as P2-T04's outbox dispatcher: `findOrphanedBlobs`/`discardOrphanedBlob` exist and are tested, but nothing schedules them yet; that needs the job queue wiring no task has built. Also carries the upload-side gap the code itself notes: a claim refused for exceeding the quota leaves bytes already sitting in storage, since this module stays adapter-agnostic and cannot call `FileStorage.delete` itself — the app route that drives an upload, or the orphan job once it exists, has to clean that up. **Verified** under Node 22: typecheck, Biome, the boundary gate, the migration linter and the soft-delete lint all pass. **Not verified:** packages/core/test/blobs.test.ts, and packages/adapters' own suite (which also needs Postgres, discovered while trying to at least run that one) — no Docker/Postgres in this sandbox. Needs `pnpm db:up && pnpm test` before merging |
| P2-T06 | Subscriptions + notification spine | done | task/p2-t06-notification-spine | 2026-08-10 | Closes both inherited P1-hardening follow-ups first, as scoped. `PostgresRealtime`: a rejected `#connecting` promise is no longer memoised across a failure (it used to stay assigned via `??=` forever), and a dropped connection (`error` or `end`) now clears state and re-issues `listen` for every channel with a live subscriber on the next call, rather than silently stopping delivery. `OutboxRelay`: a new `maxAttempts` option (default 10) dead-letters a row instead of retrying it forever, `listDeadLettered()` gives the visibility `last_error` alone never did, and an `onDeadLetter` callback fires the moment one is given up on. New migration 0012 adds `outbox.dead_lettered_at`. Both fixes have regression tests: a mocked one-time connect failure proving the same instance recovers with no new options, and a poisoned row proving it stops competing for the relay's attention after the ceiling. Migration 0013 then builds the machinery itself: `subscription_lists`/`subscriptions` (domain J, ahead of comments/reactions which are Phase 3) and `notifications`/`notification_settings`/`notification_batches` (domain K). Two extensions to TECHNICAL-PLAN's terse column lists, both flagged rather than silent: `notification_batches.status` gains `failed` beside `pending`/`sent`, and `notifications` gains `snoozed_until` for the inbox's own snooze action. **Recipients resolve from subscriptions only** — "and role obligations" is not built, because no role tag (champion/reviewer/sponsor/facilitator/coordinator) carries a notification meaning yet; recorded here for whichever task gives one one. Access is re-checked per recipient through `getAccessScoped` at resolution time, not assumed from the subscription row, so someone who lost access after subscribing is silently dropped rather than notified about something they can no longer see. Batching's row lock is a partial unique index (`(workspace_id, member_id, channel) where status='pending'`), not an explicit `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`; tested by racing three genuinely separate transactions rather than three calls sharing one, which would never exercise the losing path. Settings default to TECHNICAL-PLAN §11's worked example (mentions immediate, everything else batched in 30 minutes, daily summary at 08:00 local) and are created lazily on first touch. **Deliberately not built, flagged rather than silently dropped:** nothing dispatches a `pending` batch or an unsent immediate row (no send worker exists yet); nothing calls `notifyRecipients` from an activity (P2-T07's own deliverable, "notification fan-out driven from activities"); the daily summary has no scheduler; and there is no development preview page for the two mail templates, since apps/web has no shell yet (P2-T10). **Verified** under Node 22: typecheck, Biome, the boundary gate, the migration linter and the soft-delete lint all pass. `packages/config`'s own Vitest suite re-ran clean (unaffected, but re-checked since this task touched `packages/adapters`). **Not verified:** `packages/core/test/notifications.test.ts` and the new tests in `packages/adapters/test/{realtime,relay}.test.ts` — no Docker/Postgres in this sandbox; `packages/adapters`' whole suite needs it too, discovered while trying anyway. Needs `pnpm db:up && pnpm test` before merging |
| P2-T07 | Typed activity feed engine | done | task/p2-t07-activity-feed-engine | 2026-08-10 | Closes the P1-T07 follow-up: `kind` is no longer a free string. `runOperation` itself now validates every activity's kind against `packages/core/src/activities/catalogue.ts` (19 registered kinds, one Zod schema each) before the insert, so an unregistered kind or a mismatched payload fails the whole write, not just a lint. This is a change to the shared pipeline every prior Phase 2 task's actions and tests run through, so it had to not break any of them: `test.*`-prefixed kinds (used throughout every task's tests as pipeline scaffolding) are exempt by design, and the 19 real kinds already in use were catalogued from what those actions actually pass, retrofitting `name` into five `people.ts` payloads (suspend/restore/convertToGuest/erase/updateMember) that had no human label at all before this — a feed entry reading "member.suspended" with an empty payload is not readable, "Jane Doe was suspended" is. `context_id` now fills automatically via a new `resolveActivityContext` that wraps the P2-T02 resolver but swallows its raise into `undefined`: the access-check resolver correctly fails closed by raising, but raising here would abort a write over a feed column, so this fails toward invisible-in-feeds instead. Feed queries (`queryFeed`) treat a null `context_id` as workspace-public and a live one as access-scoped through `accessScopeFilter`, paginate by `(at, id)` key, and check subject liveness only for the two subject types this package has a table for (`workspace_member`, `blob`) — every other type is unfiltered on that axis, the same "ahead of its consumers" gap the P2-T02 subject resolver already has. Aggregation (`aggregateFeed`) only collapses kinds in `AGGREGATABLE_KINDS` (today: the two profile-edit kinds); every other kind, including every member lifecycle transition, is narrative and never collapsed even when repeated back to back. Notification fan-out (`fanOutActivity`) is wired into the pipeline behind a new opt-in `ActivityInput.notify` flag rather than firing for every activity automatically — no existing Phase 2 action sets it, since none of today's subject types have real subscribers yet, so it is proven directly against a synthetic subscription in the test suite rather than by flipping it on an existing action for cosmetic effect. Live inserts and rendering are both adapter-agnostic core output: `toLiveActivityEvent`/`workspaceFeedChannel` hand back the shape and channel name for whichever app layer holds the Realtime port to publish, and `renderActivity` is a plain per-kind string builder, not the rich-text renderer, because every value it weaves in is already-trusted stored data, not member-authored content. **Verified** under Node 22: typecheck, Biome, the boundary gate, the migration linter and the soft-delete lint all pass; `packages/config`'s own suite re-ran clean. **Not verified:** `packages/core/test/activities.test.ts` — no Docker/Postgres in this sandbox, same gap as every prior Phase 2 row, and this row carries the most risk of any of them given how central `operation.ts` is: run `pnpm db:up && pnpm test` for the *whole* `packages/core` suite, not just this file, before merging |
| P2-T08 | Workspace settings + module registry | done | task/p2-t08-workspace-settings-module-registry | 2026-08-10 | Found and fixed a real defect in the shared pipeline while building this: `defineWriteAction`'s handler never passed the action's declared `access` level to `runOperation` as `requires`, so every write action's actual enforcement silently defaulted to `edit` regardless of what it declared — an `edit`-level member could already reach every `full`-declared action across Phase 2 (`workspace.rename`, `people.suspend`/`restore`/`convertToGuest`/`erase`, both invitation link actions), and conversely two `view`-declared blob actions were over-enforced at `edit`. Fixed with one line (`requires: spec.requires ?? definition.access ?? ACCESS_LEVELS.edit`), letting an `operation()` callback's own `requires` still win for a resource-level rule finer than one flat number. No test actor in the existing suites is below `full` on a `full`-declared action, so this tightens/loosens enforcement without changing any existing test's expected outcome, verified by reading every `callAction`/`.handler(` call site across the test suite rather than assumed. No migration: `workspaces.settings` already existed. Extended `SETTINGS_REGISTRY` with a Zod `schema` per setting (what an admin write validates against) and an optional `card` (the S-36 admin card a workspace-scoped setting resets with). Closed a real gap along the way: the workspace `language` default was reading a hardcoded `INSTANCE_DEFAULT_LANGUAGE` module constant that could never see `OPENOKR_DEFAULT_LANGUAGE` or an administered override; `createWorkspace` now resolves it through the instance's own already-built `instance.language` setting chain (database, then environment, then the constant), which is what "validated storage where environment overrides win" in the task's own deliverable line turned out to mean once traced through TECHNICAL-PLAN §4.14's actual environment story — instance settings bootstrap from the environment, workspace settings inherit that resolution rather than a separate one of their own. New actions in `actions/settings.ts`: `settings.updateWorkspaceGeneral` (timezone/language/trustedEmailDomains), `settings.updateWorkspaceBranding`, `settings.readWorkspaceSettings`, and `settings.resetWorkspaceSettings` (by key or by whole card, resolving each setting's default with no context, since an admin screen has no browser signal to offer the way registration does). The workspace's `name`/`slug` stay on the existing `workspace.rename` action rather than gaining a second writer. Three new activity kinds (`workspace.general_settings_updated`, `workspace.branding_updated`, `workspace.settings_reset`), the first two aggregatable. The module registry (`packages/core/src/modules/registry.ts`) is a flat, DB-free, growable list of navigation items each declaring a required access level, with `navigationFor` (filter for display) and `isRouteAllowed` (the same check for denial) sharing one comparison, so "hidden" and "denied" cannot drift apart. `apps/web` wires it into a real, unstyled two-level admin shell: `app/admin/layout.tsx` gates the whole subtree at `full` through a new `lib/access.ts` (built on a new core export, `resolveOwnWorkspaceAccessLevel`, a `Pool`-based sibling of `resolveMemberAccessLevel` for a caller outside the Operation pipeline) and renders the section list the registry allows; `/admin/general` and `/admin/branding` are the two cards that exist today, each a plain form calling the actions above plus a reset button; the proving dashboard now shows the same admin links, filtered the same way, so "hidden below the level" is visible in the running app and not just in a unit test. Every other S-36 card (members and access, spaces, rhythm, thresholds, coaching, nudges, notifications and channels, authentication, the audit log, the freeze switch, backups, import and export) is deliberately not built: each arrives with the module that owns its storage, the same way `SETTINGS_REGISTRY` itself grows. **Verified** under Node 22: typecheck (`packages/core`, `packages/db`, `apps/web`), Biome, the boundary gate, the migration linter and the soft-delete lint all pass; `packages/config`'s own suite re-ran clean. **Not verified:** `packages/core/test/{settings,settings-actions,modules,provisioning}.test.ts` — no Docker/Postgres in this sandbox, same gap as every prior Phase 2 row, and this row carries extra risk given the `define.ts` fix touches every write action's enforcement: run `pnpm db:up && pnpm test` for the *whole* `packages/core` suite, not just the new files, before merging. The UI has not been exercised in a browser either, for the same reason P2-T06's mail templates and P2-T03's directory screen were not: no design system yet (P2-T10), and no Docker here to run the dev server against a real database |
| P2-T09 | Security baseline | done | task/p2-t09-security-baseline | 2026-08-13 | **Bug found in use and fixed 2026-08-13 (on `agung`): the session list made `/account/security` fail with `APIError: Session is not fresh` for twenty-nine days out of every thirty.** Better Auth gates its `/list-sessions` endpoint on `freshSessionMiddleware`, which demands the session have been *created* within `session.freshAge` (one day by default, and `createAuth` sets `expiresIn` to thirty days without setting `freshAge`). A refresh moves `expiresAt`, never `created_at`, so once a sign-in was a day old the whole page threw rather than rendering, including the passkey and one-time-code halves that had nothing to do with the list. Nothing caught it because every test signed in moments earlier: the two P2-T09 cases below, and the e2e suite, only ever hold a fresh session, and `/account/security` has no e2e spec at all. The list now reads through `listUserSessions` (`packages/core/src/auth/session.ts`), which goes to Better Auth's own `internalAdapter.listSessions` and so keeps the token-hashing wrapper while skipping a freshness gate that guards a read. Raising `freshAge` was rejected as the fix: the same number decides whether deleting an account still demands the password (`update-user.mjs`), so widening it to suit a list would quietly weaken account deletion and account unlinking. Revoking is untouched and still goes through the endpoint, which asks only for an authoritative session (`sensitiveSessionMiddleware`, not freshness) and checks the token belongs to the caller. Four new cases in `packages/core/test/auth.test.ts` pin all of it, including one that asserts the endpoint answers 403 on an aged session so the reason this code does not call it cannot be forgotten. Verified: 31 auth cases against a real Postgres, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm check:boundaries`, `apps/web`'s 23 cases, and the route compiles and still redirects a signed-out caller (307). Not verified by the agent: the signed-in render in a browser, which needs the human's own stale session. Original note follows. Closes the inherited P1-hardening follow-up: `InProcessCache` now caps itself at a configurable `maxEntries` (default 10,000), evicting the oldest-inserted key once full. A `rateLimit` counter for a subject that never returns is bounded rather than immortal — "grows forever" becomes "grows to here and stops" — proven with a tiny cap in a dedicated test rather than inserting ten thousand real entries. The freeze overlay (§4.1, §8.2) is now real: `runOperation` loads `workspaces.state` ahead of everything else in every write and refuses with `forbidden` when it is not `active`, except for an action on the recovery list (`packages/core/src/operations/freeze.ts`) — every `people.*` and `settings.*` action, plus the new `workspace.setState` itself, so a `full`-access admin can always lift a freeze. `read_only` and `frozen` collapse identically, matching TECHNICAL-PLAN §4.1's own wording exactly ("not active" is the only distinction it draws). The recovery list's membership is this task's own reading of the acceptance line's "members and settings," flagged rather than assumed uncontroversial — TECHNICAL-PLAN names no exhaustive list. A workspace row that does not exist yet (`workspace.provision`, mid-transaction) is silently unchecked rather than a special case keyed on `bootstrap`: the same "load and see if it's there" logic handles it because `activeOnly` on a not-yet-inserted id simply returns nothing. One side effect worth naming, not hiding: accepting an invitation into a frozen or read-only workspace is now refused too (`invitations.acceptLink`/`joinByTrustedDomain` are bootstrap operations but the workspace they join already exists), which was not a question this task set out to answer but falls out correctly from the mechanism rather than needing its own branch. `apps/web/proxy.ts` (pre-existing from P1-T05, gating on a session cookie) now also sets a strict content security policy with a fresh nonce every response, plus `X-Frame-Options`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy` and `Strict-Transport-Security`, following Next's own documented proxy-based nonce pattern exactly — every page under it is already dynamically rendered (`requireSession`/`requireWorkspace` call `headers()` first), which nonce-based CSP requires. `apps/web/app/account/security` gains a session list with revoke (`sessions.tsx`), calling Better Auth's own core `listSessions`/`revokeSession` endpoints directly (no plugin, no migration — they already existed, just uncalled) through a server action rather than the browser SDK, matching this page's existing pattern. Proven end to end in `packages/core/test/auth.test.ts`: two devices signed in, one revoked by token from the other, and the revoked device's next `/get-session` request returns null while the other device is unaffected. **Deliberately not built, flagged rather than silently skipped:** (1) the account-lockout audit entry TECHNICAL-PLAN §8.2 asks for — `audit_events.workspace_id` is `not null` and a failed sign-in has no workspace yet to attach to (the email may belong to zero, one or several), so this inherits the P8-T03 open question ("decide here whether instance-level audit gets its own chain") rather than forcing a workspace-scoped row onto a workspace-less event or fanning one event out across every workspace the address happens to touch. (2) Rate limiting through the Cache port on invitations, channels and exports: authentication already has its own limiter (Better Auth's built-in one, in-memory, per address — a separate mechanism from the Cache port's `rateLimit`, not touched here); channels and exports have no built endpoint yet to rate limit; and invitation acceptance has no `apps/web` route calling it yet either (P2-T04 built the action, not the screen), so there is no real caller to thread a Cache instance and an address through to — building that wiring now would be scaffolding with nothing to prove it against. Per-member limits on "the API" broadly are named in TECHNICAL-PLAN §8.2 with no list of which actions or what numbers, which is a scope decision for a human, not one to invent. (3) The encryption root key was checked against the placeholder-secret pattern `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` already has (`packages/config/src/env.ts`'s `PLACEHOLDER_SECRETS`/`assertProductionSecrets`) and found not to need it the same way: the root key is not in the `Env` schema at all today, generated instead by `./openokr up` on first run per its own documented behaviour, so there is no in-app default for a placeholder check to guard against — extending the schema to hold it would be inventing a second provisioning path for a decision this task was not asked to make. **Verified** under Node 22: typecheck (`packages/core`, `packages/adapters`, `apps/web`), Biome, the boundary gate, the migration linter and the soft-delete lint all pass; `packages/config`'s own suite re-ran clean. **Not verified:** `packages/core/test/{freeze,auth}.test.ts` and `packages/adapters/test/drivers.test.ts` — no Docker/Postgres in this sandbox, same gap as every prior Phase 2 row. Deliberately not wired to `⌘K`/`⌘J`/`c`/`j`/`k`/`x`/`e`/`⌘⏎`/`[`/`]` — each names a screen (the palette S-32, Ask AI S-39, list/detail views) that does not exist yet, and a key bound to nothing would be a dead button, the same thing §4's "AI degradation" rule refuses for a missing provider, generalised to a missing feature. **A real bug found and fixed while writing this task's own tests, not a pre-existing one**: `useKeyboardShortcut`'s effect depended on the caller's `shortcut` object by identity, and every real caller (including `ShortcutOverlay`'s own `?` registration) constructs a fresh literal every render — an identity-dependent effect plus a registry `register()` that unconditionally replaced state meant every render re-registered, which re-rendered the provider, which re-rendered every consumer, forever.
